(5.38)

\displaystyle\bm{\Xi}_{5321}(s,r)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{r}\left(\int_{\kappa_{r}+2\Delta}^{t}\bigg[K(\kappa_{q}+\Delta,q)\sqrt{\Delta}\mathbbm{1}_{q<t_{i}}+K(s,q)\bigg]K(\kappa_{q},u)K(\kappa_{q},r)\mathrm{d}q\right)(s-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\mathrm{d}u

(5.39)

\displaystyle=:\bm{\Xi}_{53211}(s,r)+\bm{\Xi}_{53212}(s,r).

Since, as mentioned before, the integral over the interval
(\kappa_{r}+2\Delta,t)
is null if
r\geq t_{i-1}
, we write the following decomposition for the term we want to estimate:

(5.40)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\left(\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\bm{\Xi}_{5321}(s,r)\mathrm{d}s\right)K(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r\lesssim\int_{0}^{t_{i-1}}\left(\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\big(\bm{\Xi}_{53211}(s,r)+\bm{\Xi}_{53212}(s,r)\big)\mathrm{d}s\right)K(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r,

With the definition of
\xi_{j}
in (
5.34
). The following estimates hold:

Lemma 5.29
.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{i-1}}\left(\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\bm{\Xi}_{53211}(s,r)\mathrm{d}s\right)K(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r\lesssim\Delta^{3H+1}\sum_{j=0}^{i-2}(i-j-1)^{2H}\xi_{j+1}
.

Lemma 5.30
.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{i-1}}\left(\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\bm{\Xi}_{53212}(s,r)\mathrm{d}s\right)K(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r\lesssim\Delta^{3H+\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-2}(i-j-1)^{2H}\xi_{j+1}
.

Proof of Lemma
5.29
.

With similar arguments as above, we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{i-1}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\int_{0}^{r}(s-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\Bigg\{\int_{\kappa_{r}+2\Delta}^{t}K(\kappa_{q},u)K(\kappa_{q},r)K(\kappa_{q}+\Delta,q)\sqrt{\Delta}\mathbbm{1}_{q<t_{i}}\mathrm{d}q\Bigg\}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}sK(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\Delta}\sum_{j=0}^{i-2}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}K(t_{i},r)\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\int_{0}^{r}(s-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\Bigg\{\sum_{k=j+2}^{i-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}K(\kappa_{q},u)K(\kappa_{q},r)K(\kappa_{q}+\Delta,q)\mathrm{d}q\Bigg\}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\Delta}\sum_{j=0}^{i-2}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}K(t_{i},r)\sum_{k=j+2}^{i-1}K(t_{k},r)\left(\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\int_{0}^{r}(s-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}K(t_{k},u)\left(\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}K(t_{k+1},q)\mathrm{d}q\right)\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}s\right)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta^{1+H}\sum_{j=0}^{i-2}\left(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}K(t_{i},r)\sum_{k=j+2}^{i-1}K(t_{k},r)\mathrm{d}r\right)\left(\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{j+1}}(s-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}K(t_{j+1},u)\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}s\right)

\displaystyle=:\Delta^{1+H}\sum_{j=0}^{i-2}\varpi_{j}\xi_{j+1}\lesssim\Delta^{1+3H}\sum_{j=0}^{i-2}(i-j-1)^{2H}\xi_{j+1},

where, in the very last step,
we exploited the fact that
\varpi_{j}
is bounded in the following way

(5.41)

\displaystyle\varpi_{j}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=j+2}^{i-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}K(t_{i},r)K(t_{k},r)\mathrm{d}r\lesssim K(t_{i},t_{j+1})\sum_{k=j+2}^{i-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}K(t_{k},r)\mathrm{d}r

(5.42)

\displaystyle\lesssim K(t_{i},t_{j+1})(\Delta(i-j-1))^{H+\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim(\Delta(i-j-1))^{2H}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
5.30
.

We have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{i-1}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\int_{0}^{r}(s-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\Bigg\{\int_{\kappa_{r}+2\Delta}^{t}K(\kappa_{q},u)K(\kappa_{q},r)K(s,q)\mathrm{d}q\Bigg\}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}sK(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t_{i-1}}K(t_{i},r)\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\int_{0}^{r}(s-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\Bigg\{\sum_{k=j+2}^{i}K(t_{k},u)K(t_{k},r)\left(\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}\wedge t}K(s,q)\mathrm{d}q\right)\Bigg\}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta^{H+\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-2}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}K(t_{i},r)\sum_{k=j+2}^{i-1}K(t_{k},r)\Bigg\{\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\int_{0}^{r}(s-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}K(t_{j+1},u)\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}r\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta^{H+\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-2}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}K(t_{i},r)\sum_{k=j+2}^{i-1}K(t_{k},r)\mathrm{d}r\Bigg\{\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{j+1}}(s-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}K(t_{j+1},u)\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}s\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\Delta^{H+\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-2}\varpi_{j}\xi_{j+1}\lesssim\Delta^{\frac{1}{2}+3H}\sum_{j=0}^{i-2}(i-j-1)^{2H}\xi_{j+1},

where, in the very last step,
we exploited the fact that
\varpi_{j}
can be bounded as in (
5.41
).
∎

Now, we notice that, for all
j\leq i-3
, and since
H<\frac{1}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta^{2H}(i-j-1)^{2H}=(t_{i}-t_{j+1})^{2H}\leq T^{2H}\leq(i-j-1)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}.

We must also observe that, again for
j\leq i-3
,

\displaystyle\xi_{j+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}\int_{t_{\ell}}^{t_{\ell+1}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t}K(t_{j+1},u)(s-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}\int_{t_{\ell}}^{t_{\ell+1}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t}K(t_{j+1},u)(t_{i}-t_{\ell+1})^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\Delta^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}(i-\ell-1)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\int_{t_{\ell}}^{t_{\ell+1}}K(t_{j},u)\mathrm{d}u,

whereas

\displaystyle\xi_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{i-1}\int_{t_{\ell}}^{t_{\ell+1}}K(t_{i},u)\big[(t_{i}-u)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}-(t-u)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\big]\mathrm{d}u\lesssim\Delta^{2H},

\displaystyle\xi_{i-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{i-2}\int_{t_{\ell}}^{t_{\ell+1}}K(t_{i-1},u)\big[(t_{i}-u)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}-(t-u)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\big]\mathrm{d}u\lesssim\Delta^{2H},

as they both boil down to (
4.6
) in the proof of Lemma
4.4
.

Finally, putting together the upper bound (
5.35
) and Lemmas
5.29
,
5.30
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\left\lvert\bm{\Xi}_{532}(s,r)\right\rvert\mathrm{d}sK(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\left(\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\bm{\Xi}_{5321}(s,r)\mathrm{d}s\right)K(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\left(\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\bm{\Xi}_{5322}(s,r)\mathrm{d}s\right)K(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta^{3H+\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-3}(i-j-1)^{2H}\xi_{j+1}+\Delta^{H+\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-3}(i-j-1)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{j+1}+(\Delta^{H+\frac{1}{2}}+\Delta^{3H+\frac{1}{2}})(\xi_{i-1}+\xi_{i})

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta^{H+\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-3}(i-j-1)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{j+1}+\Delta^{3H+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta^{2H}\sum_{j=0}^{i-3}(i-j-1)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}(i-\ell-1)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\int_{t_{\ell}}^{t_{\ell+1}}K(t_{j},u)\mathrm{d}u+\Delta^{3H+\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\bigstar(\Delta),

where we used Lemma
4.8
after a change of variables and
(i-j)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\leq(i-j-1)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}
.
∎

6.
Proof of Proposition
3.11

The second term to deal with is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\psi(V_{t})-\psi(V_{t_{i}})\big)\langle\partial_{\omega}(\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{t}),K^{t}\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big(\psi(V_{t})-\psi(V_{t_{i}})\big)\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big[\phi^{(2)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,t)\mathrm{d}B_{s}\Big]\bigg].

We pursue the same approach as for
\mathfrak{B}_{1}
, using Clark-Ocone formula and integration by parts. Thus, in view of this, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{r}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{D}_{r}^{W}\langle\partial_{\omega}(\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{t}),K^{t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Bigg[\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\bigg(\int_{r}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(V_{\kappa^{t}_{s}})K(\kappa^{t}_{s},r)\mathrm{d}B_{s}+\psi(V_{\kappa_{r}})\bigg)\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,t)\mathrm{d}B_{s}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{t}\Bigg[\phi^{(2)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}B_{s}\Bigg]=:\mathcal{V}_{1}(r)+\mathcal{V}_{2}(r).

We start by discussing the term
\mathcal{V}_{2}
, which requires one more application of IBP:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Bigg[\phi^{(2)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}B_{s}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\mathrm{D}_{s}^{B}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s\right]

(6.1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\bigg\{\int_{s}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(V_{v})K(v,s)\mathrm{d}B_{v}+\psi(V_{s})\bigg\}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s\right].

With a slight abuse of notations, let
\varphi_{r}(t):=\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime}(V_{t})]

(in the previous section this denoted the derivative of
\psi^{2}
).
Similarly to
\mathfrak{B}_{1}
, Clark-Ocone and integration by parts yield

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{2}(t)=\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\partial_{\omega}(\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{t}),K^{t}\rangle\big]\mathbb{E}[\psi(V_{t})-\psi(V_{t_{i}})]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{V}(r)\mathbb{E}_{r}\Big[\psi^{\prime}(V_{t})K(t,r)-\psi^{\prime}(V_{t_{i}})K(t_{i},r)\Big]\mathrm{d}r\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\partial_{\omega}(\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{t}),K^{t}\rangle\big]\mathbb{E}[\psi(V_{t})-\psi(V_{t_{i}})]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{V}_{1}(r)\mathbb{E}_{r}\Big[\psi^{\prime}(V_{t})K(t,r)-\psi^{\prime}(V_{t_{i}})K(t_{i},r)\Big]\mathrm{d}r\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)\mathbb{E}_{r}\Big[\psi^{\prime}(V_{t})K(t,r)-\psi^{\prime}(V_{t_{i}})K(t_{i},r)\Big]\mathrm{d}r\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\partial_{\omega}(\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{t}),K^{t}\rangle\big]\mathbb{E}[\psi(V_{t})-\psi(V_{t_{i}})]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{V}_{1}(r)\mathbb{E}_{r}\Big[\psi^{\prime}(V_{t})K(t,r)-\psi^{\prime}(V_{t_{i}})K(t_{i},r)\Big]\mathrm{d}r\right]

\displaystyle+\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{V}_{2}(t)\varphi_{t}(t)\right]\int_{0}^{t}\big(K(t,r)-K(t_{i},r)\big)\mathrm{d}r\right\rvert+\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{V}_{2}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\big(\varphi_{r}(t)-\varphi_{t}(t)\big)\big(K(t,r)-K(t_{i},r)\big)\mathrm{d}r\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle+\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\varphi_{r}(t)\big(\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)-\mathcal{V}_{2}(t)\big)\big(K(t,r)-K(t_{i},r)\big)\mathrm{d}r\right]\right\rvert+\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)\big(\varphi_{r}(t)-\varphi_{r}(t_{i})\big)K(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{6}\left\lvert\Upsilon_{k}\right\rvert.

The first and third terms are similar to
\bm{\Xi}_{1}
and
\bm{\Xi}_{2}
and the following mimics Lemma
5.1
:

Lemma 6.1
.

\left\lvert\Upsilon_{1}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\Upsilon_{3}\right\rvert\lesssim t^{2H}-t_{i}^{2H}
.

Lemma 6.2
.

\left\lvert\Upsilon_{2}\right\rvert\lesssim\bigstar(\Delta)
.

Sketch of proof.

The first term can be treated as
\mathcal{U}_{r}
(in the discussion for
\mathfrak{B}_{1}
) since it only differs by a smooth
L^{p}
random variable.
For the sake of preventing this proof from being too long, we do not give full details but highlight crucial points.
Whenever IBP is necessary, an additional term is generated compared to
\mathfrak{B}_{1}
.
It is however almost identical to the term that differentiates the other integral on the interval
(t,T)
, hence is treated in the same way.
Indeed, since
K(q,t)=0
for
q<t
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{q}\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,t)\mathrm{d}B_{s}=\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{s})K(s,q)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}B_{s}.

This same remark holds for higher-order Malliavin derivatives too.
When comes the time to bound the stochastic terms, the additional multiplication by the extra integral does not matter because all the terms are bounded in
L^{p}
.
∎

Now, we present the following technical lemma, which is used multiple times.

Lemma 6.3
.

For all
Y\in L^{2}
, and any
0\leq r\leq t_{i}\leq t
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|Y\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)|]\leq\int_{t}^{T}K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s
.

Proof.

Straightforward computations and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|Y\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)|]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left|Y\int_{t}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\bigg\{\int_{s}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(V_{u})K(u,s)\mathrm{d}B_{u}+\psi(V_{s})\bigg\}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})\right]K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s\right|\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left|Y\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\bigg\{\int_{s}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(V_{u})K(u,s)\mathrm{d}B_{u}+\psi(V_{s})\bigg\}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})\right]\right|\Bigg]\int_{t}^{T}K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{t}^{T}K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s.

∎

Lemma 6.4
.

\displaystyle\left\lvert\Upsilon_{4}\right\rvert\lesssim\bigstar(\Delta)
.

Proof.

As in (
5.11
)-(
5.12
), we compute
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{V}_{2}(t)(\varphi_{r}(t)-\varphi_{t}(t))\big]
by Clark-Ocone and IBP:

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{4}=\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{r}^{t}\mathrm{D}_{q}^{W}\mathcal{V}_{2}(t)\mathbb{E}_{q}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{t})]K(t,q)\mathrm{d}q\right]\big(K(t,r)-K(t_{i},r)\big)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{r}^{t}\Bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\phi^{(4)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\mathrm{D}_{q}^{W}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\mathrm{D}_{s}^{B}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\bigg\{\int_{s}^{T}\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{v})K(v,q)K(v,s)\mathrm{d}B_{v}+\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,q)\bigg\}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\mathrm{D}_{s}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{s})K(s,q)K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]\Bigg\}\mathbb{E}_{q}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{t})]K(t,q)\mathrm{d}q[K(t,r)-K(t_{i},r)]\mathrm{d}r.

In the first term, since
s\in[t,T]
,
\mathrm{D}_{s}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}
has bounded moments but the same does not hold for
\mathrm{D}_{q}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}
as
0\leq r\leq q\leq t
. Using Lemma
4.1
with
Y=\phi^{(4)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\mathrm{D}_{s}^{B}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})
then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\phi^{(4)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\mathrm{D}_{s}^{B}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})\right\rvert\left\lvert\mathrm{D}_{q}^{W}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\right\rvert\right]\lesssim\left(1+\sqrt{\Delta}K(\kappa_{q}+\Delta,q)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{q<t_{i}}.

We also apply Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the second line as follows:

(6.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert Z\right\rvert\left\lvert\int_{s}^{T}\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{v})K(v,q)K(v,s)\mathrm{d}B_{v}\right\rvert\right]^{2}

(6.3)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert Z\right\rvert^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{s}^{T}\left\lvert\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{v})K(v,q)K(v,s)\right\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}v\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert Z\right\rvert^{2}\right]\sup_{v\in[s,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{v})\right\rvert^{2}\right]K(s,q)^{2}\int_{s}^{T}K(v,s)^{2}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim K(s,q)^{2},

where
Z=\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})
.
Exploiting the fact that
1\leq K(s,q)\leq K(t,q)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\int_{r}^{t}\bigg\{\int_{t}^{T}\Big(1+\sqrt{\Delta}K(\kappa_{q}+\Delta,q)\mathbbm{1}_{q<t_{i}}\Big)K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg\}K(t,q)\mathrm{d}q\,\Big|K(t,r)-K(t_{i},r)\Big|\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\quad+3\int_{0}^{t}\int_{r}^{t}\bigg\{\int_{t}^{T}K(s,q)K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg\}K(t,q)\mathrm{d}q\,\Big|K(t,r)-K(t_{i},r)\Big|\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\int_{r}^{t_{i}}\sqrt{\Delta}K(\kappa_{q}+\Delta,q)K(t,q)\mathrm{d}q\,\Big|K(t,r)-K(t_{i},r)\Big|\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{r}^{t}K(t,q)^{2}\mathrm{d}q\,\Big|K(t,r)-K(t_{i},r)\Big|\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=:\Upsilon_{11}+\Upsilon_{12}.

By integration and Lemma
4.7
, we obtain

\Upsilon_{12}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}(t-r)^{2H}\Big(K(t_{i},r)-K(t,r)\Big)\mathrm{d}r\lesssim\bigstar(\Delta).

For
r\geq t_{i-1}
, we have
\int_{r}^{t_{i}}K(\kappa_{q}+\Delta,q)K(t,q)\mathrm{d}q\leq\int_{r}^{t_{i}}K(t_{i},q)^{2}\mathrm{d}q\lesssim\Delta^{2H}
.
Then, for
q\in[t_{\ell},t_{\ell+1})
, we have
K(t,q)\leq\big(\Delta(i-\ell-1)\big)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}
, so that, for
r<t_{i-1}
,

\displaystyle\int_{r}^{t_{i}}

\displaystyle K(\kappa_{q}+\Delta,q)K(t,q)\mathrm{d}q=\sum_{\ell=\tau(r)-1}^{i-2}\int_{t_{\ell}\vee r}^{t_{\ell+1}}K(t_{\ell+1},q)K(t,q)\mathrm{d}q+\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}K(t_{i},q)K(t,q)\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell=\tau(r)-1}^{i-2}\big(\Delta(i-\ell-1)\big)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{t_{\ell}\vee r}^{t_{\ell+1}}K(t_{\ell+1},q)\mathrm{d}q+\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}K(t_{i},q)^{2}\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta^{2H}\sum_{\ell=\tau(r)-1}^{i-2}(i-\ell-1)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}+\Delta^{2H}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta^{2H}(i-\tau(r))^{H+\frac{1}{2}}+\Delta^{2H}\lesssim\Delta^{H-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{i}-\kappa_{r})^{H+\frac{1}{2}}+\Delta^{2H}.

Finally, we conclude thanks to monotonicity of integration and Lemma
4.6

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{11}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta^{H}\int_{0}^{t_{i}}(t_{i}-\kappa_{r})^{H+\frac{1}{2}}\Big[K(t_{i},r)-K(t,r)\Big]\mathrm{d}r+\Delta^{2H+\frac{1}{2}}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\Big[K(t_{i},r)-K(t,r)\Big]\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\Delta^{H}\bigstar(\Delta)+\Delta^{1+3H}.

∎

We are left to bound
\Upsilon_{5}
and
\Upsilon_{6}
.
The first one is dealt with the following lemma.

Lemma 6.5
.

\left\lvert\Upsilon_{5}\right\rvert\lesssim\bigstar(\Delta)
.

Proof.

For all
Y\in L^{2}
and
r\leq t
, with the same computations as in Lemma
6.3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}Y\left\lvert\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)-\mathcal{V}_{2}(t)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{t}^{T}K(s,t)\big[K(s,t)-K(s,r)\big]\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{T}\big(K(s,t)^{2}-K(s,r)^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}s\lesssim(t-r)^{2H},

so that, by Lemma
4.7
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\Upsilon_{2}\right\rvert\lesssim\left\lvert\int_{0}^{t}(t-r)^{2H}\big(K(t,r)-K(t_{i},r)\big)\mathrm{d}r\right\rvert\lesssim\bigstar(\Delta)
.
∎

The last term is a tad more tedious and is detailed in the proof of the next lemma:

Lemma 6.6
.

\left\lvert\Upsilon_{6}\right\rvert\lesssim\bigstar(\Delta)
.

Proof.

Using Lemma
4.2
, we rewrite and decompose
\Upsilon_{6}
as

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{6}

\displaystyle=\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)\left(\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{q})]\int_{0}^{r}(q-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\mathrm{d}W_{u}\mathrm{d}q\right)K(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)\left(\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{(3)}(V_{q})](q-r)^{2H-1}\mathrm{d}q\right)K(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r=:\Upsilon_{61}+\Upsilon_{62}.

\mathbf{(\Upsilon_{62}})
An application of Lemma
6.3
, with
Y=\sup_{q\in[t_{i},t]}\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{(3)}(V_{q})]
, yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|Y\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)|]\lesssim\int_{t}^{T}K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s\leq\int_{t}^{T}K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s=\frac{(T-t)^{2H}}{2H},

that in turn, by Lemma
4.4
, entails

\displaystyle\left\lvert\Upsilon_{62}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\left(\int_{t_{i}}^{t}(q-r)^{2H-1}\mathrm{d}q\right)K(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\left|(t-r)^{2H}-(t_{i}-r)^{2H}\right|K(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r\lesssim\bigstar(\Delta).

\mathbf{(\Upsilon_{61}})
This term requires IBP with respect to
W
. In particular, we rewrite it as

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{61}=\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\int_{0}^{r}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathrm{D}^{W}_{u}\Big(\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{q})]\Big)\Big](q-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}qK(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r.

For all
u\in[0,r)
, we recall that

(6.4)

\mathrm{D}_{u}^{W}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}=\int_{u}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(V_{\kappa^{t}_{q}})K(\kappa_{q}^{t},u)\mathrm{d}B_{q}+\rho\psi(V_{\kappa^{t}_{u}}),

and, combined with the equivalent representation of
\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)
in (
6
), we obtain

(6.5)

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{u}^{W}\Big(\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{q})]\Big)

(6.6)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\phi^{(4)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\mathrm{D}_{u}^{W}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\mathrm{D}_{s}^{B}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s\right]\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{q})]

(6.7)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\left(\int_{s}^{T}\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{v})K(v,u)K(v,s)\mathrm{d}B_{v}\right)\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s\right]\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{q})]

(6.8)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,u)\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s\right]\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{q})]

(6.9)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\mathrm{D}_{s}^{B}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{s})K(s,u)K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s\right]\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{q})]

(6.10)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\mathrm{D}_{s}^{B}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s\right]\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{(3)}(V_{q})]K(q,u),

where in the second term
K(u,v)=0
since
u\leq r\leq t\leq s\leq v
.

In order to bound
\mathrm{D}_{u}^{W}(\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{q})])
as per (
6.5
),
we apply Lemma
4.1
to the first line (
6.5
) with

Y=\phi^{(4)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\mathrm{D}_{s}^{B}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{q})]
, which entails

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\phi^{(4)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{q})]\mathrm{D}_{s}^{B}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})\right\rvert\left\lvert\mathrm{D}_{u}^{W}X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}}\right\rvert\right]\lesssim\big(1+\sqrt{\Delta}K(\kappa_{u}+\Delta,u)\big).

We also apply Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the second line (
6.5
) as in (
6.2
), yielding

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\phi^{(3)}(X_{T}^{t,\overline{X}_{t}})\psi^{\prime}(V_{s})\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{q})]\right\rvert\left\lvert\int_{s}^{T}\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{v})K(v,u)K(v,s)\mathrm{d}B_{v}\right\rvert\right]^{2}\lesssim K(s,u)^{2}.

For the last three lines we simply observe that the stochastic terms are uniformly bounded in
L^{p}
and
K(s,u)\leq K(q,u)
, hence we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathrm{D}_{u}\Big(\mathcal{V}_{2}(r)\mathbb{E}_{r}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(V_{q})]\Big)\Big]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{t}^{T}\left\{1+\sqrt{\Delta}K(\kappa_{u}+\Delta,u)\right\}K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s+4K(q,u)\int_{t}^{T}K(s,r)K(s,t)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=:\Upsilon_{611}(u,r)+\Upsilon_{612}(u,r,q).

\mathbf{(\Upsilon_{611}})

Exploiting again
K(s,r)\leq K(s,t)
, we get that
\Upsilon_{611}(u,r)\lesssim 1+\sqrt{\Delta}K(\kappa_{u}+\Delta,u)
.
This term then boils down to (
5.30
), which gives us

(6.11)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r}\Upsilon_{611}(u,r)(q-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\mathrm{d}u\lesssim\int_{0}^{r}\Big(1+\sqrt{\Delta}K(\kappa_{u}+\Delta,u)\Big)(q-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\mathrm{d}u\lesssim\Delta^{1+H}.

\mathbf{(\Upsilon_{612}})
Since
K(s,r)\leq K(s,t)
, by Lemma
4.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\int_{0}^{r}\Upsilon_{612}(u,r,q)(q-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}qK(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t}\int_{0}^{r}\left(K(q,u)\int_{t}^{T}K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)(q-u)^{H-\frac{3}{2}}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}qK(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t_{i}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t}(q-r)^{2H-1}\mathrm{d}qK(t_{i},r)\mathrm{d}r\lesssim\bigstar(\Delta).

∎

7.
Proofs of Section
2.4

7.1.
Proof of Lemma
2.21

Let
p>0
.
First,
V
is a Gaussian process hence
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{p\left\lVert V\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}}\right]
is finite
[
22
, Lemma 6.13]
, proving the first bound of (
2.27
).
Now, by Gaussian computations,

\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{pV_{s}^{t,\omega}}\right]=\mathrm{e}^{p\omega_{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(p\int_{t}^{s}K(t,r)\,\mathrm{d}W_{r}\right)\right]=\exp\left(p\omega_{t}+\frac{p^{2}(s-t)^{2H}}{4H}\right),

yielding the second estimate.
For the last one, by BDG and Jensen’s inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\left\lvert X_{s}^{t,x,\omega}\right\rvert^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert x\right\rvert^{p}+\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\left(\int_{t}^{s}\psi(r,V_{r}^{t,\omega})\,\mathrm{d}B_{r}\right)^{p}+\zeta^{p}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\psi(r,V_{r}^{t,\omega})^{2}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert x\right\rvert^{p}+\mathfrak{b}_{p}\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi(r,V_{r}^{t,\omega})^{2}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{p/2}+\zeta^{p}T^{p-1}\int_{t}^{T}\left\lvert\psi(r,V_{r}^{t,\omega})\right\rvert^{2p}\mathrm{d}r\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert x\right\rvert^{p}+\mathfrak{b}_{p}T^{p/2-1}\int_{0}^{T}\left\lvert\psi(r,V_{r})\right\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}s+\zeta^{p}T^{p-1}\int_{0}^{T}\left\lvert\psi(r,V_{r})\right\rvert^{2p}\mathrm{d}r\right],

where
\mathfrak{b}_{p}
is the BDG constant. Furthermore, Assumption
2.19
(ii) gives

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left\lvert\psi(r,V_{r})\right\rvert^{2p}\mathrm{d}r\right]\leq\sup_{r\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{2p{\kappa_{\psi}}\left(r+V^{t,\omega}_{r}\right)}\right].

This, combined with the second estimate, yields the claim.
By assumption there exists
\ell>0
such that

G(X_{t},V_{t})\lesssim 1+\left\lvert X_{t}\right\rvert^{\ell}+\mathrm{e}^{\ell V_{t}}
.
From (
2.27
) that
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert G(X,V)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\right]
is then finite.

7.2.
Proof of Proposition
2.22

1
1

1

The authors would like to thank Jean-François Chassagneux for pointing out this nice method of proof.

For all
(s,(t,x,\omega))\in\mathbb{T}\times\overline{\Gamma}
, such that
t<s
, recall
V^{t,\omega}_{s}
and
X_{T}^{t,x,\omega}
defined in (
2.26
).

\bullet
We start by considering the derivatives in
x
and for clarity we note
X^{x}_{T}=X_{T}^{t,x,\omega}
, with
(t,\omega)
fixed. For any
\varepsilon>0
, notice that
X^{x+\varepsilon}_{T}-X^{x}_{T}=\varepsilon
hence

(7.1)

\phi(X_{T}^{x+\varepsilon})-\phi(X_{T}^{x})=(X^{x+\varepsilon}_{T}-X^{x}_{T})\int_{0}^{1}\phi^{\prime}(\lambda X^{x+\varepsilon}_{T}+(1-\lambda)X^{x}_{T})\mathrm{d}\lambda=\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\phi^{\prime}(X^{x}_{T}+\lambda\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}\lambda.

Since
\phi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}^{1}
it is also locally Lipschitz continuous which entails

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,\omega}\left[\frac{\phi(X_{T}^{x+\varepsilon})-\phi(X_{T}^{x})}{\varepsilon}-\phi^{\prime}(X^{x}_{T})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t,\omega}\left[\int_{0}^{1}\phi^{\prime}(X^{x}_{T}+\lambda\varepsilon)-\phi^{\prime}(X^{x}_{T})\mathrm{d}\lambda\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{t,\omega}\left[\sup_{\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\varepsilon)}\left\lvert\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{x+\varepsilon^{\prime}}_{T})\right\rvert\right]\int_{0}^{1}\lambda\varepsilon\mathrm{d}\lambda,

which tends to zero as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
by Assumption
2.19
(i) and (
2.27
), as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,\omega}\left[\sup_{\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\varepsilon)}\left\lvert\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{x+\varepsilon^{\prime}}_{T})\right\rvert\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t,\omega}\left[\sup_{\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\varepsilon)}\left\lvert\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{x}_{T}+\varepsilon^{\prime})\right\rvert\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{t,\omega}\left[\sup_{\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\varepsilon)}(1+|X^{x}_{T}+\varepsilon^{\prime}|^{\kappa_{\phi}})\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\sup_{\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\varepsilon)}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\kappa_{\phi}}+\mathbb{E}_{t,\omega}\left[|X^{x}_{T}|^{\kappa_{\phi}}\right]<\infty.

This proves the expression of the first derivative.
The second space derivative is proved in the same fashion since
\phi^{\prime\prime}
also belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{1}
by assumption.

\bullet

We turn to the Fréchet derivative; by a mild abuse of notation we will write
\eta
instead of
\eta\mathbbm{1}_{[t,T]}
in the remainder of this proof.
We fix
(t,x)
and write (
2.26
) as
X^{\omega}_{T}
. For clarity, we only show the proof in the case
\zeta=0
, the general case being analogous. Recall the definition of the pathwise derivative in (
2.3
), thus the proof amounts to showing that

\lim_{\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\downarrow 0}\frac{u(t,x,\omega+\eta)-u(t,x,\omega)-\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\phi^{\prime}(X_{T})\left\{\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right\}\right]}{\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}}=0.

We then write

(7.2)

\phi(X_{T}^{\omega+\eta})-\phi(X_{T}^{\omega})=\delta_{\eta}X^{\omega}_{T}\,\widetilde{\phi}(\eta),

where
\delta_{\eta}X^{\omega}_{T}:=X^{\omega+\eta}_{T}-X^{\omega}_{T}
and
\widetilde{\phi}(\eta):=\int_{0}^{1}\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T}+\lambda\delta_{\eta}X^{\omega}_{T})\mathrm{d}\lambda
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}}\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\phi(X_{T}^{\omega+\eta})-\phi(X_{T}^{\omega})-\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right]

(7.3)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}}\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\widetilde{\phi}(\eta)\left(\delta_{\eta}X^{\omega}_{T}-\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right]

(7.4)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}}\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\left(\widetilde{\phi}(\eta)-\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})\right)\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right].

To conclude, we need to show that the terms on the right-hand side tend to zero as
\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}
goes to zero. We can thus restrict to
\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\leq 1
. Let us start by considering the first one.
On the one hand, for all
\lambda\in(0,1)
, Assumption
2.19
gives

(7.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\big[\phi^{\prime}(\lambda X^{\omega}_{T}+(1-\lambda)X^{\omega+\eta}_{T})^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\left\lvert X^{\omega}_{T}\right\rvert^{2{\kappa_{\phi}}}+\left\lvert X^{\omega+\eta}_{T}\right\rvert^{2{\kappa_{\phi}}}\right],

which is bounded by (
2.27
) and in turn yields the finiteness of the second moment of
\widetilde{\phi}(\eta)
.
Moreover, we notice that
V^{\omega+\eta}_{s}-V^{\omega}_{s}=\eta_{s}
which yields

(7.6)

\displaystyle\delta_{\eta}X^{\omega}_{T}

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}\psi(s,V^{\omega+\eta}_{s})-\psi(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}

(7.7)

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}\left((V^{\omega+\eta}_{s}-V^{\omega}_{s})\int_{0}^{1}\psi^{\prime}\big(s,V^{\omega}_{s}+\lambda(V^{\omega+\eta}_{s}-V^{\omega}_{s})\big)\,\mathrm{d}\lambda\right)\mathrm{d}B_{s}

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}\eta_{s}\widetilde{\psi}_{s}(\eta)\mathrm{d}B_{s},

where
\widetilde{\psi}_{s}(\eta):=\int_{0}^{1}\psi^{\prime}\big(s,V^{\omega}_{s}+\lambda\eta_{s}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\lambda
.
Now, the local Lipschitz continuity of
\psi^{\prime}(s,\cdot)
gives

(7.8)

\widetilde{\psi}_{s}(\eta)-\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})=\int_{0}^{1}\Big[\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s}+\lambda\eta_{s})-\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\Big]\mathrm{d}\lambda\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\left\lVert\tau\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\leq 1}\left\lvert\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s}+\tau_{s})\right\rvert|\eta_{s}|.

For all
p>1
, again by Assumption
2.19
and (
2.27
), we have

(7.9)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\sup_{\left\lVert\tau\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\leq 1}\left\lvert\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s}+\tau_{s})\right\rvert^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\sup_{\left\lVert\tau\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\leq 1}(1+\mathrm{e}^{{\kappa_{\psi}}(V^{\omega}_{s}+\tau_{s})})^{p}\right]\lesssim 1+\sup_{\left\lVert\tau\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\leq 1}\mathrm{e}^{{\kappa_{\psi}}p\tau_{s}}\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\mathrm{e}^{{\kappa_{\psi}}pV^{\omega}_{s}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\mathrm{e}^{{\kappa_{\psi}}p}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{{\kappa_{\psi}}pV^{\omega}_{s}}\right]<\infty.

This allows us to show, exploiting (
7.6
) and Itô’s isometry first and then (
7.8
),

(7.10)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\left(\delta_{\eta}X^{\omega}_{T}-\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\left(\int_{t}^{T}\big(\widetilde{\psi}_{s}(\eta)-\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\big)\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\big(\widetilde{\psi}_{s}(\eta)-\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\big)^{2}\eta_{s}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]

(7.11)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}^{2}\sup_{s\in\mathbb{T}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t,x}[\sup_{\left\lVert\tau\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\leq 1}\left\lvert\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s}+\tau_{s})\right\rvert^{2}](T-t)}{4},\qquad\qquad

which goes to zero as
\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}
tends to zero.
By virtue of Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the sentence below (
7.5
) and (
7.11
), we conclude that the first term (
7.3
) tends to zero.

For the second term, by definition of
\widetilde{\phi}
, the local Lipschitz continuity of
\phi^{\prime\prime}
, Assumption
2.19
and (
2.27
), we have

(7.12)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}(\eta)-\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\big(\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T}+\lambda\delta_{\varepsilon\eta}X^{\omega}_{T})-\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})\big)\mathrm{d}\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\alpha\in(0,1)}\left\lvert\phi^{\prime\prime}\left((1-\alpha)X^{\omega}_{T}+\alpha X^{\omega+\varepsilon\eta}_{T}\right)\right\rvert\int_{0}^{1}\lambda\left\lvert\delta_{\varepsilon\eta}X^{\omega}_{T}\right\rvert\mathrm{d}\lambda

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(1+\left\lvert X^{\omega}_{T}\right\rvert^{\kappa_{\phi}}+\left\lvert X^{\omega+\varepsilon\eta}_{T}\right\rvert^{\kappa_{\phi}}\right)\left\lvert\delta_{\varepsilon\eta}X^{\omega}_{T}\right\rvert.

By (
7.6
), BDG and Hölder’s inequality, we have

(7.13)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[(\delta_{\varepsilon\eta}X^{\omega}_{T})^{4}\right]\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}\int_{t}^{T}\eta_{s}^{4}\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\widetilde{\psi}_{s}(\varepsilon)^{4}\right]\mathrm{d}s,

while Assumption
2.19
(ii) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s}+\lambda\varepsilon\eta_{s})^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\mathrm{e}^{4{\kappa_{\psi}}T}\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\exp\left(4{\kappa_{\psi}}(V^{\omega}_{s}+\lambda\varepsilon\eta_{s})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\mathrm{e}^{4{\kappa_{\psi}}\left(\omega_{s}+\lambda\varepsilon\eta_{s}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\mathrm{e}^{4{\kappa_{\psi}}I^{t}_{s}}\right],

which is uniformly bounded for
s,t\in\mathbb{T}
since
I_{t}^{s}
is Gaussian and, as a consequence, so is
\mathbb{E}_{t,x}[\widetilde{\psi}_{s}(\varepsilon)^{4}]
.
Using Cauchy-Schwarz and
(1+\left\lvert X^{\omega}_{T}\right\rvert^{\kappa_{\phi}}+\left\lvert X^{\omega+\varepsilon\eta}_{T}\right\rvert^{\kappa_{\phi}})\in L^{4}
, we conclude that

\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\left(\widetilde{\phi}(\varepsilon)-\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})\right)^{2}\right]=0.

On the other hand, similar computations yield

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)^{2}\right]<\infty.

A new application of Cauchy-Schwarz and the last two displays show that the second term (
7.4
) tends to zero as well, concluding the proof of the first pathwise derivative formula.
The crossed derivative
\partial_{\omega x}u
is computed analogously since
\phi^{\prime\prime}
is also Lipschitz continuous.

\bullet
For the second pathwise derivative, we again consider only the case
\zeta=0
, as the other follow with the same method.
First of all, let us recall that we use the same notations as in the previous bullet point with
(t,x)
fixed.
For all
\varepsilon>0,\eta^{(1)},\eta^{(2)}\in\mathcal{W}_{t},\omega\in\mathcal{W}
, let us define

\Psi_{t}^{i}(\omega):=\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\eta^{(i)}_{s}\mathrm{d}B_{s},

for
i=1,2
,
so that we are interested in the difference

(7.14)

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega+\eta^{(2)}}_{T})\Psi_{t}^{1}(\omega+\eta^{(2)})-\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})\Psi_{t}^{1}(\omega)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\left\{\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega})\Psi_{t}^{1}(\omega)\Psi_{t}^{2}(\omega)+\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega})\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right\}

(7.15)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\left\{\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega+\eta^{(2)}}_{T})-\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})-\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega})\Psi_{t}^{2}(\omega)\right\}\Psi_{t}^{1}(\omega)

(7.16)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega+\eta^{(2)}}_{T})\left\{\Psi_{t}^{1}(\omega+\eta^{(2)})-\Psi_{t}^{1}(\omega)-\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right\}

(7.17)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\left\{\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega+\eta^{(2)}}_{T})-\phi^{\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})\right\}.

Once again, we need to take expectations, divide by
\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}
and show that the limit as
\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\downarrow 0
is zero.
The first term (
7.15
) can be dealt with in the same way as (
7.4
) with
\phi^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}
replaced by
\phi^{\prime\prime},\psi^{\prime\prime}
and one more application of Cauchy-Schwarz to separate the term in brackets and
\Psi_{t}^{1}(\omega)
. The third term (
7.17
)
is studied similarly to (
7.2
) exploiting (
7.6
).
We focus our attention on the second term (
7.16
).
As in (
7.6
), we start by writing

\Psi^{1}_{t}(\omega+\eta^{(2)})-\Psi^{1}_{t}(\omega)=\int_{t}^{T}\big(\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega+\eta^{(2)}}_{s})-\psi^{\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\big)\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}=\int_{t}^{T}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{s}(\eta^{(2)})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s},

where
\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{s}(\eta^{(2)}):=\int_{0}^{1}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s}+\lambda\varepsilon\eta^{(2)}_{s})\mathrm{d}\lambda
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\Psi^{1}_{t}(\omega+\eta^{(2)})-\Psi^{1}_{t}(\omega)

\displaystyle-\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\mathrm{d}B_{s}

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}\left(\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{s}(\eta^{(2)})-\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\right)\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\mathrm{d}B_{s}

(7.18)

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\left(\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s}+\lambda\eta^{(2)}_{s})-\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V^{\omega}_{s})\right)\mathrm{d}\lambda\right)\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\mathrm{d}B_{s}.

Now, as in (
7.11
), Itô’s isometry for (
7.2
) and the local Lipschitz continuity of
\psi^{\prime\prime}
yield

\displaystyle\lim_{\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}}\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\left(\Psi^{1}_{t}(\omega+\eta^{(2)})-\Psi^{1}_{t}(\omega)-\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)^{2}\right]=0,

which concludes the proof.

7.3.
Proof of Proposition
2.23

We recall that the pathwise derivatives with respect to a singular direction is the limit of smooth directions, as in (
2.5
).
Hence, we introduce the smooth approximation
K^{\delta,t}:=K(\cdot\vee(t+\delta),t)
which belongs to
\mathcal{W}_{t}
, such that Proposition
2.22
holds with
\eta=K^{\delta,t}
, and then we pass to the limit
\delta\downarrow 0
.
We show that the conclusions of Proposition
2.22
still hold for the second pathwise derivative, and in the case
\zeta=0
, as the first derivative and the other cases follow in a straightforward way using the same techniques.

\bullet
Let us start with the first term of (
2.31
). Note first that
K^{\delta,t}-K^{t}=0
on
[t+\delta,T]
. We use the identity
a^{2}-b^{2}=(a+b)(a-b)
and apply Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

(7.19)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\phi^{\prime}(X_{T})\left(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})K(t+\delta,t)\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)^{2}\right]

(7.20)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\phi^{\prime}(X_{T})\left(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})K(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)^{2}\right]

(7.21)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\phi^{\prime}(X_{T})^{2}\left\{\left(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})K(t+\delta,t)\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)^{2}+\left(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})K(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)^{2}\right\}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(7.22)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\left(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})\big\{K(t+\delta,t)-K(s,t)\big\}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We study the second term in (
7.21
).
For all
p\geq 2
,
BDG and Holder’s inequalities yield

(7.23)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\left\lvert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}K(s,t)\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right\rvert^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{b}_{p}\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\left\lvert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}K(s,t)^{2}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{\frac{p}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{b}_{p}\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\left\lvert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\big(K(s,t)^{\frac{4}{p}}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})^{2}\big)K(s,t)^{\frac{2p-4}{p}}\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{\frac{p}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{b}_{p}\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\left\lvert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\big(K(s,t)^{\frac{4}{p}}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})^{2}\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathrm{d}r\right\rvert\cdot\left\lvert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}K(s,t)^{\frac{2p-4}{p}\frac{p}{p-2}}\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{\frac{p}{2}\frac{p-2}{p}}\right]

(7.24)

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{b}_{p}\int_{t}^{t+\delta}K(s,t)^{2}\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}[\left\lvert\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})\right\rvert^{p}]\mathrm{d}s\left(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}.

Since
\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}[\left\lvert\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})\right\rvert^{p}]=\mathbb{E}[\left\lvert\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t,\omega})\right\rvert^{p}]
is uniformly bounded in
s
, the above is bounded by some constant times
(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s)^{p/2}\leq C\delta^{pH}
.
A similar reasoning holds replacing
K(s,t)
with
K(t+\delta,t)
, hence Cauchy-Schwarz ensures that (
7.21
) is bounded. Itô’s isometry, together with Assumption
2.19
and (
2.28
), yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\left(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})\big\{K(t+\delta,t)-K(s,t)\big\}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})^{2}\big\{K(t+\delta,t)-K(s,t)\big\}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\sup_{s\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}[\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s})^{2}]\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\big\{K(t+\delta,t)-K(s,t)\big\}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\delta^{2H}.

\bullet
The other term to deal with is, for
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\phi^{\prime}(X_{T})\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s})K(s\vee(t+\delta),t)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s})K(s\vee(t+\delta),t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right],

using Malliavin integration by parts (
2.30
). Otherwise, when taking
\delta\downarrow 0
, we would obtain the ill-defined integral
\int_{t}^{T}K(s,t)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}
. Notice that, for
r\leq s
,
\mathrm{D}_{s}^{W}V_{r}=K(s,r)
and
\mathrm{D}_{s}^{\overline{W}}V_{r}=0
.
Therefore,
\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T})=\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T})\big(\mathrm{D}_{s}^{W}X_{T},\mathrm{D}^{\overline{W}}_{s}X_{T}\big)^{\top}
where

(7.25)

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{\overline{W}}_{s}X_{T}

\displaystyle=\overline{\rho}\psi(s,V_{s}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{s}^{W}X_{T}

\displaystyle=\rho\psi(s,V_{s})+\int_{s}^{T}\mathrm{D}_{s}\psi(r,V_{r})\,\mathrm{d}B_{r}+\zeta\int_{0}^{T}\mathrm{D}_{s}^{W}\psi(r,V_{r})^{2}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\rho\psi(s,V_{s})+\int_{s}^{T}K(r,s)\psi^{\prime}(r,V_{r})\,\mathrm{d}B_{r}+\zeta\int_{s}^{T}K(r,s)(\psi^{2})^{\prime}(r,V_{r})\mathrm{d}r.

Since
\psi,\psi^{2},(\psi^{2})^{\prime}
have subexponential growth,
the following holds for all
p>1
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[|\psi(s,V_{s})|^{p}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\left\lvert\int_{s}^{T}K(r,s)(\psi^{2})^{\prime}(r,V_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\rvert^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim 1+\mathrm{e}^{p{\kappa_{\psi}}T}\sup_{s\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\mathrm{e}^{p{\kappa_{\psi}}V_{s}}\right]+\mathrm{e}^{p\kappa_{\psi}^{2}T}\sup_{s\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\mathrm{e}^{p\kappa_{\psi}^{2}V_{s}}\right]\left(\int_{s}^{T}K(r,s)\mathrm{d}r\right)^{p}.

By virtue of (
2.27
) and
K\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
, these terms are uniformly bounded in
L^{p}
so that the first and the last terms on the right-hand side of (
7.25
) are in
L^{p}
.
The same technique as in (
7.24
) shows the same holds for the second term in the second equation in (
7.25
); this entails

(7.26)

\sup_{s\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\right\rvert^{p}\right]<\infty.

Hence the proof follows from the estimate

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s})\Big\{K(s\vee(t+\delta),t)^{2}-K(s,t)^{2}\big\}\mathrm{d}s\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}_{t,x,\omega}\left[\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s})\right]\left\lvert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\Big\{K(t+\delta,t)^{2}-K(s,t)^{2}\big\}\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert\lesssim\delta^{2H}.

7.4.
Proof of Proposition
2.24

As in Definition
2.7
, let
(t,x,\omega)\in\overline{\Gamma}
,
\eta,\eta^{(1)},\eta^{(2)}\in\mathcal{W}_{t}
with supports in
[t,t+\delta]
for some small
\delta>0
. Essentially, the estimates in
x,\omega
are verified thanks to growth conditions on
\phi,\psi
and the bounds (
2.27
). The presence of
\eta
in a Riemann integral (resp. stochastic integral) leads to a bound proportional to
\delta
(resp.
\sqrt{\delta}
). This justifies the estimates of Definition
2.7
with
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}
. Finally the continuity of the derivatives is a consequence of the regularity of
\phi
and
\psi
.

(i)

Since
\phi^{\prime}
and
\phi^{\prime\prime}
both have polynomial growth, it is clear from (
2.27
) that
\partial_{x}u
and
\partial_{xx}u
have
G
-growth as introduced in Definition
2.7
.
Let us note these derivatives do not require the factor
\delta^{\alpha}
as they are not in the direction of a singular kernel.
Turning to the pathwise derivative
\langle\partial_{\omega}u,\eta\rangle
given in Proposition
2.22
, we apply Itô’s isometry after Hölder’s inequality and by the growth of
\phi,\psi
and (
2.27
), there exist
G_{1},G_{2}\in\mathcal{X}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t,x,\omega})\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t,\omega})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t,x,\omega})^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t,\omega})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert G_{1}(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t,\omega})^{2}\eta_{s}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert G_{1}(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\sup_{s\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}\left[\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t,\omega})^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\sqrt{\delta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert G_{1}(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert G_{2}(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\,\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\sqrt{\delta}.

Here
\left\lVert G(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{T}}\left\lvert G(x,\omega_{t})\right\rvert
.
Clearly,
G_{1}G_{2}
is also in
\mathcal{X}
by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and the second term of
\langle\partial_{\omega}u,\eta\rangle
can be dealt with in the same way by applying Jensen’s inequality instead of Itô’s isometry. This shows the
G
-growth with
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}
. The case of
\langle\partial_{\omega}(\partial_{x}u),\eta\rangle
is identical.

For the second pathwise derivative, we exploit the representation (
2.31
). From (
7.26
),

\int_{t}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t,x,\omega}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\right]\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\int_{t}^{T}\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta_{s}^{(2)}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\left\lVert\eta^{(1)}_{s}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta^{(2)}_{s}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\delta.

Similar computations show that for some
G\in\mathcal{X}

\mathbb{E}\left[\zeta\int_{t}^{T}(\psi_{s}^{2})^{\prime\prime}(V_{s}^{t,\omega})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]\lesssim\left\lVert G(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}^{2}\,\left\lVert\eta^{(1)}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta^{(2)}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\delta.

For the other terms, we apply Hölder’s inequality with
\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{p_{2}}+\frac{1}{p_{3}}=1
to separate the three factors and there exist
G\in\mathcal{X}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{t,x,\omega})\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t,\omega})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t,\omega})\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{t,x,\omega})\right\rVert_{L^{p_{1}}}\left\lVert\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t,\omega})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right\rVert_{L^{p_{2}}}\left\lVert\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t,\omega})\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right\rVert_{L^{p_{3}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert G(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta^{(1)}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta^{(2)}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\delta,

where the last inequality follows from BDG inequality and taking the supremum of
\eta^{(1)},\eta^{(2)}
.
The same technique of splitting into three different factors shows that the other terms also yield the same estimates.
This concludes Definition
2.7
(i) with
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}
.

(ii)
For the regularity, we consider
(t,x,\omega),(t,x^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\in\overline{\Gamma}
. We focus on
\omega
-continuity as
x
-continuity is easier and follows with similar arguments. Hence we fix
(t,x)
, abbreviate
X^{t,x,\omega}
by
X^{\omega}
and denote the
L^{2}
norm under
\mathbb{E}_{t,x}
as
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x}

\displaystyle\left[\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega})\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}})\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\left(\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega})-\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right)\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}})\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}-\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega})-\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\left\lVert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\left\lVert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\left(\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})-\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right)\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}.

Previous computations showed there exists
G\in\mathcal{X}
such that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert G(x,\omega^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}},

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}},

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert G(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\sqrt{\delta}.

Similarly to (
7.6
) we have

\left\lVert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\left(\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})-\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right)\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\lesssim\left\lVert G(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\sqrt{\delta}.

Starting with a decomposition similar to the one in (
7.6
), then applying Itô’s isometry and the fact that
\eta
is uniformly bounded over time together with Assumption
2.19
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\left(\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})-\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right)\eta_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\eta_{s}(V_{s}^{\omega}-V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\int_{0}^{1}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,\lambda V_{s}^{\omega}+(1-\lambda)V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\mathrm{d}\lambda\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\eta_{s}^{2}(\omega_{s}-\omega^{\prime}_{s})^{2}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,\lambda V_{s}^{\omega}+(1-\lambda)V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\mathrm{d}\lambda\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\int_{0}^{1}(1+\mathrm{e}^{\kappa_{\psi}(\lambda V_{s}^{\omega}+(1-\lambda)V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})})^{2}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left(1+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{2\kappa_{\psi}I^{t}_{s}}\right]\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{e}^{2\kappa_{\psi}(\lambda\omega_{s}+(1-\lambda)\omega^{\prime}_{s})}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left(1+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}(\mathrm{e}^{2\kappa_{\psi}\omega_{s}}+\mathrm{e}^{2\kappa_{\psi}\omega^{\prime}_{s}})\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\left\lVert G(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}+\left\lVert G(x,\omega^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}})\left\lVert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\sqrt{\delta},

where we used the convexity of the exponential.
Following the idea of (
7.2
), we also know by local Lipschitz continuity and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega})-\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert X^{\omega}_{T}-X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T}\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\left\lVert\int_{0}^{1}\phi^{\prime\prime}(\lambda X^{\omega}_{T}+(1-\lambda)X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T})\mathrm{d}\lambda\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\left\lVert G(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}+\left\lVert G(x,\omega^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}})\left\lVert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}.

where, to get the bound for the integral, we have exploited the fact that, by Assumption
2.19
, monotonicity and an application of Lemma
2.21
, we have

(7.27)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{0}^{1}\phi^{\prime\prime}(\lambda X^{\omega}_{T}+(1-\lambda)X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T})\mathrm{d}\lambda\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert\int_{0}^{1}(1+(\lambda X^{\omega}_{T}+(1-\lambda)X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T})^{\kappa_{\phi}})\mathrm{d}\lambda\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}

(7.28)

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\left\lVert\int_{0}^{1}(\lambda X^{\omega}_{T})^{\kappa_{\phi}}+((1-\lambda)X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T})^{\kappa_{\phi}}\mathrm{d}\lambda\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}

(7.29)

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\left\lVert(X^{\omega}_{T})^{\kappa_{\phi}}\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}+\left\lVert(X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T})^{\kappa_{\phi}}\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}

(7.30)

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+(|x|^{4\kappa_{\phi}}+\mathrm{e}^{2\kappa_{\psi}4\kappa_{\psi}\left\lVert\omega\right\rVert_{T}})^{\frac{1}{4}}+(|x|^{4\kappa_{\phi}}+\mathrm{e}^{2\kappa_{\psi}4\kappa_{\psi}\left\lVert\omega^{\prime}\right\rVert_{T}})^{\frac{1}{4}}

(7.31)

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+|x|^{4\kappa_{\phi}}+\mathrm{e}^{2\kappa_{\psi}4\kappa_{\psi}\left\lVert\omega\right\rVert_{T}}+\mathrm{e}^{2\kappa_{\psi}4\kappa_{\psi}\left\lVert\omega^{\prime}\right\rVert_{T}},

which corresponds to
\left\lVert G(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}+\left\lVert G(x,\omega^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}
in the bound above.
Once again, the other term follows from the same steps.

Let us move on to the second derivative with representation (
2.31
).
The first term in the representation is handed in the following way.
Let us notice once more that without loss of generality we restrict our study to the case
\zeta=0
, as the additional terms are studied with similar computations.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\mathrm{d}s\right]-\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\Bigg[\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\Bigg\{\Big(\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega}),\bm{\rho}\rangle-\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\Big)\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\left(\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})-\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right)\Bigg\}\eta^{(1)}_{s}\eta^{(2)}_{s}\mathrm{d}s\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\left\lVert\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega}),\bm{\rho}\rangle-\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\left\lVert\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\mathrm{d}s\left\lVert\eta^{(1)}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta^{(2)}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\left\lVert\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\left\lVert\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})-\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\mathrm{d}s\left\lVert\eta^{(1)}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta^{(2)}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}.

Concerning the second term on the right-hand side, we have already seen that
\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}}),\bm{\rho}\rangle
is bounded in
L^{2}
and, since
\psi^{\prime\prime}
is
\mathcal{C}^{1}
,
\left\lVert\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})-\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left\lVert G(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}
.
On the other hand, for the first term, we see from (
7.25
) that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega}),\bm{\rho}\rangle-\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

(7.32)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\rho\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})\mathrm{D}_{s}X^{\omega}_{T}+\bar{\rho}\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})\overline{\mathrm{D}}_{s}X^{\omega}_{T}-\rho\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T})\mathrm{D}_{s}X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T}+\bar{\rho}\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T})\overline{\mathrm{D}}_{s}X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

and, focusing on the first term, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality yields

(7.33)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})\mathrm{D}_{s}X^{\omega}_{T}-\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T})\mathrm{D}_{s}X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

(7.34)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\big(\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})-\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T})\big)\mathrm{D}_{s}X^{\omega}_{T}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}+\left\lVert\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T})\big(\mathrm{D}_{s}X^{\omega}_{T}-\mathrm{D}_{s}X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T}\big)\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

(7.35)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{\omega}_{T})-\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T})\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\left\lVert\mathrm{D}_{s}X^{\omega}_{T}\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}+\left\lVert\phi^{\prime\prime}(X^{\omega^{\prime}}_{T})\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\bigg[\left\lVert\psi(s,V_{s}^{\omega})-\psi(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}

(7.36)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\int_{s}^{T}K(r,s)\big(\psi^{\prime}_{r}(V_{r}^{\omega})-\psi^{\prime}_{r}(V_{r}^{\omega^{\prime}})\big)\mathrm{d}B_{r}\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}+\left\lVert\int_{s}^{T}K(r,s)\big((\psi^{2}_{r})^{\prime}(V_{r}^{\omega})-(\psi^{2}_{r})^{\prime}(V_{r}^{\omega^{\prime}})\big)\mathrm{d}r\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\bigg].

The regularity of
\psi,\psi^{\prime},\psi^{\prime\prime}
allow us to conclude, using similar computations as before, that this term is smaller than
\left\lVert G(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta^{(1)}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta^{(2)}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\delta
.

For the next term, we separate this triple in the following way

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega})\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}})\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\left(\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega})-\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right)\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}})\left(\int_{t}^{T}\left(\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})-\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right)\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{t,x}\left[\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{\omega^{\prime}})\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\left(\int_{t}^{T}\left(\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega})-\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{\omega^{\prime}})\right)\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right],

and then applying Hölder to each of them, the same arguments as in point (i) show that is bounded by
\left\lVert G(x,\omega)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta^{(1)}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lVert\eta^{(2)}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{T}}\delta
.

(iii)

We are left checking the last condition, continuity of the pathwise derivatives on
\overline{\Gamma}
.
We just showed uniform continuity in
\omega
,
thus time continuity is the only step left.
We only show it for the second derivative, using (
2.31
). We fix
x,\omega
; as in point (ii) of this proof,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{t+\delta})\left(\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\left(\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{t})\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right]

(7.37)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\left(\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{t+\delta})-\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{t})\right)\left(\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\left(\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right]\qquad

(7.38)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{t})\left(\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}-\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\left(\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right]\qquad\qquad

(7.39)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{t})\left(\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\left(\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}-\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})\eta^{(2)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)\right].\qquad\qquad

For the first line (
7.37
), we apply Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to focus on the difference

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{t+\delta})-\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{T}^{t})\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{x,\omega}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert X_{T}^{t+\delta}-X_{T}^{t}\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{x,\omega}}\left\lVert\int_{0}^{1}\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda X_{T}^{t+\delta}+(1-\lambda)X_{T}^{t})\,\mathrm{d}\lambda\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{x,\omega}}.

Furthermore, by BDG and Jensen inequalities

\displaystyle\left\lVert X_{T}^{t+\delta}-X_{T}^{t}\right\rVert_{L^{4}_{x,\omega}}^{4}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\left(\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\big(\psi(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})-\psi(s,V_{s}^{t})\big)\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi(s,V_{s}^{t})\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}\right)^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8\mathfrak{b}_{4}T\int_{t}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\left\lvert\psi(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})-\psi(s,V_{s}^{t})\right\rvert^{4}\right]\mathrm{d}s+8\delta\mathfrak{b}_{4}\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\psi(s,V_{s})^{4}\right]\mathrm{d}s,

where the second term clearly goes to zero as
\delta
tends to zero.
For the first one, we write

(7.40)

\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\left\lvert\psi(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})-\psi(s,V_{s}^{t})\right\rvert^{4}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\left\lvert\left(V_{s}^{t+\delta}-V_{s}^{t}\right)\int_{0}^{1}\psi^{\prime}\left(s,V^{t}_{s}+\lambda\left(V_{s}^{t+\delta}-V_{s}^{t}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}\lambda\right\rvert^{4}\right],

where the integral is bounded in
L^{8}
as in (
2.27
) and

\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\left\lvert V_{s}^{t+\delta}-V_{s}^{t}\right\rvert^{8}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\left\lvert\int_{t}^{t+\delta}K(s,r)\,\mathrm{d}W_{r}\right\rvert^{8}\right]\leq\mathfrak{b}_{8}\left(\int_{t}^{t+\delta}K(s,r)^{2}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{4}\leq\mathfrak{b}_{8}\delta^{8H}.

Yet another application of Cauchy-Schwarz yields that (
7.37
) goes to zero with
\delta
.

The second and third lines (
7.38
) and (
7.39
) are identical hence we only deal with the second one.
We decompose further

\displaystyle\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}-\int_{t}^{T}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}

\displaystyle=\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\big(\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})-\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})\big)\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})\eta^{(1)}_{s}\,\mathrm{d}B_{s}

The integral between
t
and
t+\delta
was already studied point (ii) and we showed these tend to zero in
L^{p}
norm as
\delta\downarrow 0
. Since
\psi^{\prime}
is also
\mathcal{C}^{1}
, the same computations as above show that
\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}[\left\lvert\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})-\psi^{\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})\right\rvert^{4}]
goes to zero, which concludes the time continuity of the first term of the representation in (
2.31
).

Regarding the next term,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t+\delta}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})K(s,t+\delta)^{2}\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t}^{T}\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\bigg[\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\Big\{\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t+\delta}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})K(s,t+\delta)^{2}-\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})K(s,t)^{2}\Big\}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\Big\{\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t+\delta}),\bm{\rho}\rangle-\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\Big\}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})K(s,t+\delta)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\Big\{\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t+\delta})-\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})\Big\}K(s,t+\delta)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})\Big\{K(s,t+\delta)^{2}-K(s,t)^{2}\Big\}\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right].

Using the expression (
7.25
) and similar techniques as before, the first term boils down to evaluating differences of the type (
7.40
), albeit with
\psi^{\prime}
instead of
\psi
, which we proved tends to zero as
\delta\downarrow 0
. The second term follows identically with
\psi^{\prime\prime}
this time. For the third one we apply Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to isolate the term

\left\lvert\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\Big(K(s,t+\delta)^{2}-K(s,t)^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert\lesssim\delta^{2H}.

Finally, the last term is smaller in absolute value than

\sup_{s\in[t,t+\delta]}\mathbb{E}_{x,\omega}\left[\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\phi^{\prime}(X_{T}^{t}),\bm{\rho}\rangle\psi^{\prime\prime}(s,V_{s}^{t})\right]\int_{t}^{t+\delta}K(s,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\delta^{2H}.

The other terms of (
2.31
) can be shown to be continuous using the same techniques. This concludes Definition
2.7
(iii) and hence the proof of the proposition.
